## GRB-357 · L3 · hard · Strengthening and weakening an argument · officer

Argument: Online classes are as effective as classroom teaching, since the online batch's average score equalled the classroom batch's.

Which of the following, if true, most weakens the argument?

- **A.** Both batches followed the same syllabus and used the same textbooks.  _(error: strengthens comparability)_
- **B.** The online classes were delivered through a mix of recorded and live video sessions.  _(error: irrelevant)_
- **C.** Classroom sessions for the other batch were held in the morning hours on weekdays.  _(error: irrelevant)_
- **D.** Students chose their batch, and the online group had scored far higher the previous year. ✅

**Working**

1. A stronger online group only equalling the other suggests online teaching was less effective.

**Formula:** Selection bias  
**Trap:** Check that the groups compared were alike to begin with.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## GRB-358 · L3 · hard · Strengthening and weakening an argument · officer

Argument: A retailer's sales fell last quarter because a competitor opened stores nearby.

Which of the following, if true, most strengthens the argument?

- **A.** The competitor's founder is a well-known and widely admired figure in the retail industry.  _(error: irrelevant)_
- **B.** Overall consumer spending in the country fell sharply during the same quarter.  _(error: rival explanation — weakens)_
- **C.** Sales fell only at outlets near the competitor's new stores; other outlets held steady. ✅
- **D.** The retailer mainly sells groceries and household goods at its outlets across the city.  _(error: irrelevant)_

**Working**

1. The fall is localised exactly where the competitor opened — supports the cause.

**Formula:** Localised effect supports a specific cause  
**Trap:** Economy-wide factors would affect all outlets.

- **B.** South-East  _(error: opposite direction (reversed the reference point))_

**Working**

1. Place Q at (0, 0): P = (0, 6), R = (8, 0).

## GRB-369 · L3 · hard · Direction of one point relative to another · officer

One morning, soon after sunrise, A and B were facing each other. A's shadow fell exactly to A's right. Which direction was B facing?

- **B.** East  _(error: shadow direction or side confused)_
- **C.** West  _(error: shadow direction or side confused)_
- **D.** South  _(error: shadow direction or side confused)_

**Working**

1. The sun is in the East, so shadows fall to the West.
2. West is on A's right → A faces South.
3. B faces A → faces North.

**Formula:** Morning shadow → West; evening shadow → East  
**Trap:** Facing each other or back to back, the two people face opposite directions.

2. Gap: (8, -27) → √793 m.

**Formula:** √(Δx² + Δy²)  
**Trap:** Work out both end points from the same origin before comparing.

## GRB-420 · L3 · hard · Two-person path problems · officer

Arjun starts from the same point S and walks 7 m towards the South, then turns left and walks 3 m, then turns right and walks 4 m, then turns left and walks 5 m. Vikram starts from the same point S and walks 6 m towards the West, then turns right and walks 3 m, then turns right and walks 4 m, then turns right and walks 12 m. In which direction is Arjun with respect to Vikram now? (North-East, South-West etc. denote the quadrant between the two main directions.)

- **A.** South-West  _(error: turned the wrong way once)_
- **B.** North-West  _(error: opposite direction (reversed the reference point))_
- **C.** North-East  _(error: turned the wrong way once (other side))_
- **D.** South-East ✅

**Working**

1. Arjun ends at (8, -11); Vikram ends at (-2, -9).
2. Arjun − Vikram = (10, -2) → South-East.

**Formula:** Direction of A from B = sign of (xA − xB, yA − yB)  
**Trap:** Work out both end points from the same origin before comparing.

- **B.** V is the tallest. ✅
- **C.** Z is shorter than X.  _(error: possible but not certain)_
- **D.** W is the second tallest.  _(error: possible but not certain)_

**Working**

1. 4 orders are possible, e.g. V > W > X > Y > Z; V > W > X > Z > Y; V > W > Z > X > Y.
2. Only 'V is the tallest.' holds in all of them.

**Formula:** A statement is definitely true only if it holds in every possible order  
**Trap:** Z's position is not fixed relative to W, X, Y.

**Formula:** Chain the comparisons into one order

## GRB-481 · L1 · easy · Arithmetic operation machine · foundation

- **A.** 43 18 86 62 76  _(error: stopped one step early)_
- **B.** 14 9 9 12 8  _(error: skipped Step I)_
- **C.** 13 8 14 9 7  _(error: wrote the output in reverse order)_
- **D.** 7 9 14 8 13 ✅

**Working**

1. Step I: 43 18 86 62 76
2. Step II: 7 9 14 8 13

**Formula:** Apply the stated operation to every number, one step at a time  
**Trap:** Apply each step to the output of the previous step, not to the original input.

## GRB-486 · L4 · hard · Arithmetic operation machine · officer

Numbers in a row are processed from left to right: the first two numbers give a result, which then works with the third number, and so on. Rules: (i) an even number followed by an odd number — add them; (ii) an odd number followed by an odd number — multiply them; (iii) an odd number followed by an even number — take the difference (larger minus smaller); (iv) an even number followed by an even number — take half of their sum.

Row 1: 10 8 17
Row 2: 10 5 18
Row 3: 13 13 10
Row 4: 8 12 7

## GRB-511 · L2 · medium · Shifting and rearrangement machine · foundation

A machine rearranges a line of numbers. In each step, the smallest number not yet arranged moves to the leftmost position among the unarranged numbers, and the others shift right. Convention: a step moves exactly the elements named by the rule; a step in which nothing would change is not counted, and the machine stops as soon as the arrangement is complete.
Input: 82 79 81 63 71 35
What is Step II?

- **A.** 35 82 79 81 63 71  _(error: stopped one step early (this is the previous step))_
- **B.** 82 81 79 71 63 35  _(error: moved the largest number instead)_
- **C.** 35 63 82 79 81 71 ✅
- **D.** 35 63 71 82 79 81  _(error: went one step too far (this is the next step))_

**Working**

1. Step I: 35 82 79 81 63 71
2. Step II: 35 63 82 79 81 71

**Formula:** Simulate the machine one step at a time  
**Trap:** Each step builds on the previous step's arrangement, not on the input.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:
